Well, yeah, I guess I have. It's funny you mention hobbies, because I used to be out in the yard every evening. I've got these tomato plants, right? Big, beautiful beefsteaks. I started growing them from seed back in March, kept 'em under a heat lamp in the garage before the last frost. My neighbor, Bill, he always tries to grow them too, but he uses that cheap store-bought soil and wonders why his end up looking all scraggly. I keep telling him he needs to compost, but he never listens. 

But lately... I don't know, I just look out the window at them and it's like they belong to somebody else. Same thing with my workbench. I used to love sanding down old scrap wood, just making things smooth, you know? And football—I've been a Falcons fan since I was a boy. Had season tickets back in the nineties. But this past Sunday, I didn't even turn the game on. Even at church, I used to stay after and talk with folks for an hour, but now I just slip out the back as soon as the benediction is over. It's just... none of it feels like mine anymore.

Tired? No, I can't say I've noticed that. I've got about as much energy as I usually do. I do drink a fair amount of coffee, though. Just regular black coffee from the breakroom. Gary actually just bought us one of those new machines, you know, the ones that take the little plastic pods? I told him it was a waste of money, but he insisted. The old glass pot we had kept leaking everywhere. I swear, I was getting the mop out three times a week just to clean up the linoleum. And let me tell you, the mops they send us from corporate now are just pure garbage. They just push the dirty water around instead of soaking it up. It's like trying to clean up a spill with a plastic bag. Back in the day, we had these thick cotton mop heads that could dry a whole puddle in one go... but, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being tired. Yeah, physically I feel fine, my energy hasn't really dropped or anything like that.

Depressed? No, I wouldn't use that word. I'm not sitting around crying or feeling sorry for myself. Crying never did anyone any good anyway. My old man used to say that. He worked at the steel mill up in Birmingham for forty years. Forty years, breathing in all that dust and heat. He never complained once. Well, except about the price of gas. Man, I remember when gas went over a dollar a gallon, he practically blew a gasket. He drove this old Ford pickup—it used to be candy apple red, but it was all rusted out in the wheel wells by the time I was a teenager. He loved that truck, though. Kept the engine pristine. He used to make me hold the flashlight for him out in the dirt driveway while he tinkered with the carburetor, and if I didn't hold the beam exactly where he needed it, he'd just holler and holler... 

But anyway, what were you asking? Oh, feeling down. No, I'm not really feeling sad or down like that. I just don't care about my tomatoes or the Falcons right now, like I said, but I'm not walking around moping all day.

Man, I remember when gas went over a dollar a gallon, he practically blew a gasket. He drove this old Ford pickup—it used to be candy apple red, but it was all rusted out in the wheel wells by the time I was a teenager. He loved that truck, though. Kept the engine pristine. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I definitely haven't been restless. It's actually the opposite. People at work keep asking me if I'm coming down with something. They say I'm walking slower, and honestly, it feels like I'm moving through mud just trying to get from my desk to the warehouse floor. And taking forever to answer simple questions, too. Gary asked me about the inventory logs yesterday, and I just stared at him for a solid minute before I could even get a word out. 

Speaking of Gary, he's been on this new health kick lately. Drinking these green smoothies that smell like fresh cut grass. He keeps trying to get me to try one, says it'll give me more energy. I told him I'd rather eat dirt. My wife, she tried one of those juice cleanses back in '08, right before our daughter's wedding. She was so cranky for three days, I ended up sleeping on the couch just to get some peace. The couch isn't bad, actually. It's one of those big sectionals from Rooms To Go. We got it on sale on Labor Day. The delivery guys, though, they scuffed up the doorframe trying to get it inside. I had to get the wood putty out and fix it myself. I used to be pretty handy with that stuff, but like I said before, I haven't even looked at my workbench lately... 

Wait, what was the question? Oh, moving slower. Yeah, like I said, it feels like I'm walking through mud, and everyone at work is noticing.

Sleep? No, I've been sleeping my regular hours. Usually out like a light by 10:30. My wife, on the other hand, she's a very light sleeper. We actually had to get one of those sound machines, you know, the ones that play ocean waves or a rainstorm? I think it just sounds like static on an old tube TV. Remember those? The big heavy wooden boxes that took up half the living room. 

My granddad had this huge Zenith console TV down in his den. Thing weighed an absolute ton. When the picture tube finally blew, he wouldn't let anyone throw it out. He just put a lace doily on top of it and used it as a plant stand. He had these massive Boston ferns that would drop little dried leaves everywhere. My grandma was always chasing after him with the Dustbuster. Man, those old Dustbusters... the batteries on those things were terrible. You'd leave it plugged into the wall all week, and you'd get maybe two minutes of suction before it started whining like a dying mosquito. They don't make appliances like they used to, that's for sure. Now everything is just cheap plastic from overseas. 

Anyway... wait, what did you ask me? Did you ask about my wife's sleep? Oh, right, my sleep. Yeah, no, my sleep is fine. I'm not waking up early or sleeping the day away or anything like that. Just regular sleep.

I'd say this whole change in me, these thoughts and all, it's been going on for about two weeks. Right around the time my daughter left Georgia. She just shipped off for the military, you see. Army. I told her, I said, \"Sarah, are you sure about this? The military is a tough place for a young woman.\" But she's stubborn, just like her mother was. God rest her soul. Sarah's always been the kind to just make up her mind and go, ever since she was a little girl. I remember this one time, she must have been, oh, maybe eight years old, and she decided she was going to build a treehouse all by herself with some scrap wood I brought home from the warehouse... But yeah, it's been about two weeks since she left.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you usually enjoy, like work, hobbies, or spending time with family?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I have. It's funny you mention hobbies, because I used to be out in the yard every evening. I've got these tomato plants, right? Big, beautiful beefsteaks. I started growing them from seed back in March, kept 'em under a heat lamp in the garage before the last frost. My neighbor, Bill, he always tries to grow them too, but he uses that cheap store-bought soil and wonders why his end up looking all scraggly. I keep telling him he needs to compost, but he never listens. \n\nBut lately... I don't know, I just look out the window at them and it's like they belong to somebody else. Same thing with my workbench. I used to love sanding down old scrap wood, just making things smooth, you know? And football—I've been a Falcons fan since I was a boy. Had season tickets back in the nineties. But this past Sunday, I didn't even turn the game on. Even at church, I used to stay after and talk with folks for an hour, but now I just slip out the back as soon as the benediction is over. It's just... none of it feels like mine anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling more tired or having less energy lately, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: Tired? No, I can't say I've noticed that. I've got about as much energy as I usually do. I do drink a fair amount of coffee, though. Just regular black coffee from the breakroom. Gary actually just bought us one of those new machines, you know, the ones that take the little plastic pods? I told him it was a waste of money, but he insisted. The old glass pot we had kept leaking everywhere. I swear, I was getting the mop out three times a week just to clean up the linoleum. And let me tell you, the mops they send us from corporate now are just pure garbage. They just push the dirty water around instead of soaking it up. It's like trying to clean up a spill with a plastic bag. Back in the day, we had these thick cotton mop heads that could dry a whole puddle in one go... but, uh, what were we talking about? No, I wouldn't use that word. I'm not sitting around crying or feeling sorry for myself. Crying never did anyone any good anyway. My old man used to say that. He worked at the steel mill up in Birmingham for forty years. Forty years, breathing in all that dust and heat. He never complained once. Well, except about the price of gas. Man, I remember when gas went over a dollar a gallon, he practically blew a gasket. He drove this old Ford pickup—it used to be candy apple red, but it was all rusted out in the wheel wells by the time I was a teenager. He loved that truck, though. Kept the engine pristine. He used to make me hold the flashlight for him out in the dirt driveway while he tinkered with the carburetor, and if I didn't hold the beam exactly where he needed it, he'd just holler and holler... \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Oh, feeling down. No, I'm not really feeling sad or down like that. It's actually the opposite. People at work keep asking me if I'm coming down with something. Drinking these green smoothies that smell like fresh cut grass. He keeps trying to get me to try one, says it'll give me more energy. I told him I'd rather eat dirt. I had to get the wood putty out and fix it myself. I used to be pretty handy with that stuff, but like I said before, I haven't even looked at my workbench lately... \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, moving slower. Yeah, like I said, it feels like I'm walking through mud, and everyone at work is noticing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble sleeping lately, like waking up early or not being able to fall asleep, or on the flip side, have you been sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I've been sleeping my regular hours. Usually out like a light by 10:30. My wife, on the other hand, she's a very light sleeper. When the picture tube finally blew, he wouldn't let anyone throw it out. He just put a lace doily on top of it and used it as a plant stand. Focus? Yeah, it's been real tough lately. Down at the distribution center, I'll be sitting there staring at the schedule or the routing sheet, and my mind just won't lock in. Even little staffing calls, like figuring out who's covering the loading dock, they feel way bigger than they ought to. It's like I can't put the pieces together. 

It reminds me of when my youngest, Bobby, was trying to put together this Lego pirate ship I got him for his tenth birthday. Must have had a thousand pieces. Little tiny gray and black bricks scattered all over the living room rug. That rug was a nightmare to keep clean, by the way. Shag carpeting. Who thought that was a good idea? My wife picked it out, said it made the room look "cozy." Cozy until you step on a Lego barefoot at two in the morning going for a glass of water. I swear, stepping on a Lego is worse than stepping on a rusty nail. I did that once, actually, stepped on a nail out by the old shed. Had to go get a tetanus shot. The nurse who gave it to me, she had the heaviest hand I've ever felt. My arm ached for a week. I couldn't even lift my bowling ball. We used to bowl every Thursday night down at Pin Strikes. I had a custom ball, swirl of blue and silver, weighed exactly sixteen pounds...

What were we talking about? Oh, right, thinking clearly. Yeah, like I said, I just can't seem to focus on the schedules at work. My brain just won't lock onto what I'm looking at.

Even at church, I used to stay after and talk with folks for an hour, but now I just slip out the back as soon as the benediction is over. It's just... none of it feels like mine anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling more tired or having less energy lately, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: Tired? No, I can't say I've noticed that. I've got about as much energy as I usually do. I do drink a fair amount of coffee, though. Just regular black coffee from the breakroom. Gary actually just bought us one of those new machines, you know, the ones that take the little plastic pods? I told him it was a waste of money, but he insisted. The old glass pot we had kept leaking everywhere. I swear, I was getting the mop out three times a week just to clean up the linoleum. And let me tell you, the mops they send us from corporate now are just pure garbage. They just push the dirty water around instead of soaking it up. It's like trying to clean up a spill with a plastic bag. Back in the day, we had these thick cotton mop heads that could dry a whole puddle in one go... but, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being tired. Yeah, physically I feel fine, my energy hasn't really dropped or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Depressed? No, I wouldn't use that word. I'm not sitting around crying or feeling sorry for myself. Crying never did anyone any good anyway. My old man used to say that. He worked at the steel mill up in Birmingham for forty years. Forty years, breathing in all that dust and heat. He never complained once. Well, except about the price of gas. Man, I remember when gas went over a dollar a gallon, he practically blew a gasket. He drove this old Ford pickup—it used to be candy apple red, but it was all rusted out in the wheel wells by the time I was a teenager. He loved that truck, though. Kept the engine pristine. He used to make me hold the flashlight for him out in the dirt driveway while he tinkered with the carburetor, and if I didn't hold the beam exactly where he needed it, he'd just holler and holler... \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Oh, feeling down. No, I'm not really feeling sad or down like that. I just don't care about my tomatoes or the Falcons right now, like I said, but I'm not walking around moping all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving slower than usual or, on the contrary, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Well, I definitely haven't been restless. It's actually the opposite. People at work keep asking me if I'm coming down with something. Gary asked me about the inventory logs yesterday, and I just stared at him for a solid minute before I could even get a word out. \n\nSpeaking of Gary, he's been on this new health kick lately. Drinking these green smoothies that smell like fresh cut grass. He keeps trying to get me to try one, says it'll give me more energy. I told him I'd rather eat dirt. My wife, she tried one of those juice cleanses back in '08, right before our daughter's wedding. She was so cranky for three days, I ended up sleeping on the couch just to get some peace. The couch isn't bad, actually. It's one of those big sectionals from Rooms To Go. We got it on sale on Labor Day. The delivery guys, though, they scuffed up the doorframe trying to get it inside. I had to get the wood putty out and fix it myself. I used to be pretty handy with that stuff, but like I said before, I haven't even looked at my workbench lately... \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, moving slower. Yeah, like I said, it feels like I'm walking through mud, and everyone at work is noticing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble sleeping lately, like waking up early or not being able to fall asleep, or on the flip side, have you been sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I've been sleeping my regular hours. Usually out like a light by 10:30. My wife, on the other hand, she's a very light sleeper. We actually had to get one of those sound machines, you know, the ones that play ocean waves or a rainstorm? I think it just sounds like static on an old tube TV. Remember those? The big heavy wooden boxes that took up half the living room. \n\nMy granddad had this huge Zenith console TV down in his den. Thing weighed an absolute ton. When the picture tube finally blew, he wouldn't let anyone throw it out. He just put a lace doily on top of it and used it as a plant stand. He had these massive Boston ferns that would drop little dried leaves everywhere. My grandma was always chasing after him with the Dustbuster. Man, those old Dustbusters... the batteries on those things were terrible. You'd leave it plugged into the wall all week, and you'd get maybe two minutes of suction before it started whining like a dying mosquito. They don't make appliances like they used to, that's for sure. Now everything is just cheap plastic from overseas. \n\nAnyway... wait, what did you ask me? Did you ask about my wife's sleep? Even little staffing calls, like figuring out who's covering the loading dock, they feel way bigger than they ought to. It's like I can't put the pieces together. \n\nIt reminds me of when my youngest, Bobby, was trying to put together this Lego pirate ship I got him for his tenth birthday. I swear, stepping on a Lego is worse than stepping on a rusty nail. I did that once, actually, stepped on a nail out by the old shed. Had to go get a tetanus shot. The nurse who gave it to me, she had the heaviest hand I've ever felt. My arm ached for a week. I couldn't even lift my bowling ball. We used to bowl every Thursday night down at Pin Strikes. I had a custom ball, swirl of blue and silver, weighed exactly sixteen pounds...\n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right, thinking clearly. Yeah, like I said, I just can't seem to focus on the schedules at work. It's actually the opposite. People at work keep asking me if I'm coming down with something. They say I'm walking slower, and honestly, it feels like I'm moving through mud just trying to get from my desk to the warehouse floor. And taking forever to answer simple questions, too. Gary asked me about the inventory logs yesterday, and I just stared at him for a solid minute before I could even get a word out. \n\nSpeaking of Gary, he's been on this new health kick lately. Drinking these green smoothies that smell like fresh cut grass. He keeps trying to get me to try one, says it'll give me more energy. I told him I'd rather eat dirt. My wife, she tried one of those juice cleanses back in '08, right before our daughter's wedding. She was so cranky for three days, I ended up sleeping on the couch just to get some peace. Oh, moving slower. Yeah, like I said, it feels like I'm walking through mud, and everyone at work is noticing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble sleeping lately, like waking up early or not being able to fall asleep, or on the flip side, have you been sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I've been sleeping my regular hours. Usually out like a light by 10:30. My wife, on the other hand, she's a very light sleeper. We actually had to get one of those sound machines, you know, the ones that play ocean waves or a rainstorm? I think it just sounds like static on an old tube TV. Remember those? The big heavy wooden boxes that took up half the living room. \n\nMy granddad had this huge Zenith console TV down in his den. Thing weighed an absolute ton. When the picture tube finally blew, he wouldn't let anyone throw it out. He just put a lace doily on top of it and used it as a plant stand. He had these massive Boston ferns that would drop little dried leaves everywhere. My grandma was always chasing after him with the Dustbuster. Man, those old Dustbusters... the batteries on those things were terrible. You'd leave it plugged into the wall all week, and you'd get maybe two minutes of suction before it started whining like a dying mosquito. They don't make appliances like they used to, that's for sure. Now everything is just cheap plastic from overseas. \n\nAnyway... wait, what did you ask me? Did you ask about my wife's sleep? Oh, right, my sleep. Yeah, no, my sleep is fine. I'm not waking up early or sleeping the day away or anything like that. Just regular sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder than usual to focus on tasks, make decisions, or think clearly over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Focus? Yeah, it's been real tough lately. Down at the distribution center, I'll be sitting there staring at the schedule or the routing sheet, and my mind just won't lock in. Even little staffing calls, like figuring out who's covering the loading dock, they feel way bigger than they ought to. It's like I can't put the pieces together. \n\nIt reminds me of when my youngest, Bobby, was trying to put together this Lego pirate ship I got him for his tenth birthday. Must have had a thousand pieces. Little tiny gray and black bricks scattered all over the living room rug. That rug was a nightmare to keep clean, by the way. Shag carpeting. Who thought that was a good idea? My wife picked it out, said it made the room look \"cozy.\" Cozy until you step on a Lego barefoot at two in the morning going for a glass of water. I swear, stepping on a Lego is worse than stepping on a rusty nail. I did that once, actually, stepped on a nail out by the old shed. Had to go get a tetanus shot. The nurse who gave it to me, she had the heaviest hand I've ever felt. My arm ached for a week. I couldn't even lift my bowling ball. We used to bowl every Thursday night down at Pin Strikes. I had a custom ball, swirl of blue and silver, weighed exactly sixteen pounds...\n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right, thinking clearly. Yeah, like I said, I just can't seem to focus on the schedules at work. My brain just won't lock onto what I'm looking at.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or weight recently?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 57 / general_or_operations_manager / less_than_9th\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Weight_Appetite_Change\nmanifestation: \"Most days I have to make myself eat a few bites, and even food I normally like at the warehouse or at home just sits there until I throw it out.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Appetite? Well, I haven't really had one, to be honest. Most days I have to force myself just to eat a few bites. Even the stuff I usually like, you know, the sandwiches my wife packs or the chili from the food truck outside the warehouse, it just sits there on my desk until I end up throwing it out. 

Speaking of that food truck, the guy who runs it, Sal, he's been parking in the exact same spot by the loading dock for going on five years now. He’s got this bright yellow truck with a giant smiling taco painted on the side. The paint's starting to peel, though. You can see the old logo underneath, used to be an ice cream truck, I think. I remember when the ice cream man used to come through our neighborhood when I was a kid. You'd hear that little tinkling music from three blocks away and just take off running. My mom would give me a quarter, and that was enough for a Bomb Pop and a handful of those little wax bottles with the colored sugar water inside. I don't even know what that liquid was, probably pure chemicals. Nobody cared back then. We used to ride our bikes without helmets, drink from the garden hose... the hose water always tasted a little bit like rubber and dirt, but it was the best water you ever had when it was ninety degrees out. We had this big oak tree in the front yard we'd sit under to cool off. Lightning struck it back in '92, split the trunk right down the middle. Had to pay a guy five hundred bucks to come chop it up and haul it away. Five hundred bucks was a lot of money back then...

Wait, did you ask about my weight? Or food? Right, eating. Yeah, I just have to make myself eat lately, and most of it just goes in the trash.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Have I had a period like this before? Well, looking back on it, these low stretches... they've been coming around for about a year now. Just these times where I don't care much. But this one, right after my daughter left, this one has hit me the hardest. 

She just moved out to Oregon, you know. Got a job with some tech company out in Portland. It's a long way from here. We drove out there to help her move, me and the wife. Rented one of those big yellow Penske trucks. Driving that thing through the mountains was nerve-wracking, I'll tell you what. The brakes on those rental trucks always feel a little spongy to me. We stopped at this diner somewhere in Idaho, place looked like a giant potato from the outside. Best chicken fried steak I've ever had, though. The gravy was thick, real peppery, just how I like it. My doctor's always telling me to watch my cholesterol, lay off the gravy and the red meat. Says I need to eat more leafy greens. I tried eating kale once. Tasted like dirt and cardboard mixed together. I don't understand how people eat that stuff on purpose. My neighbor, Gary, he grows it in his backyard. Gary's a nice guy, but he's always out there at 6 AM on a Saturday with his leaf blower. Who uses a leaf blower at 6 AM on a weekend? People are trying to sleep. I went over there once to ask him to keep it down, and he started talking my ear off about his new riding mower...

Anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the low stretches. Yeah, they've been happening for about a year, but this time, since she moved, it's just been really, really tough to care about anything.

How can I help you today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor... I guess I'm here because I ended up having a talk with my supervisor, Gary. Gary's a good guy, you know? We've been running the floor together for, gosh, must be fifteen years now. Back when the company was just a single warehouse over on 4th street, before corporate bought us out. Now we got three facilities and it's a massive headache keeping track of all the inventory, especially with the new kids they hire who can't even drive a forklift right without knocking over a pallet... \n\nBut, uh, anyway, where was I? Right, Gary. I told him because I got pretty spooked. I just... I keep having these thoughts lately that maybe my family would be better off if I just went to sleep and didn't wake up. It scared the hell out of me, honestly, having that run through my head. So I told Gary, and he told me I needed to come here. Which is tough because we got a huge shipment coming in today and I really shouldn't even be away from the dock right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have these thoughts been happening to you?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I'd say this whole change in me, these thoughts and all, it's been going on for about two weeks. Right around the time my daughter left Georgia. She just shipped off for the military, you see. Army. I told her, I said, \"Sarah, are you sure about this? The military is a tough place for a young woman.\" But she's stubborn, just like her mother was. God rest her soul. Sarah's always been the kind to just make up her mind and go, ever since she was a little girl. I remember this one time, she must have been, oh, maybe eight years old, and she decided she was going to build a treehouse all by herself with some scrap wood I brought home from the warehouse... But yeah, it's been about two weeks since she left.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you usually enjoy, like work, hobbies, or spending time with family?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I have. It's funny you mention hobbies, because I used to be out in the yard every evening. I've got these tomato plants, right? Big, beautiful beefsteaks. I started growing them from seed back in March, kept 'em under a heat lamp in the garage before the last frost. My neighbor, Bill, he always tries to grow them too, but he uses that cheap store-bought soil and wonders why his end up looking all scraggly. I keep telling him he needs to compost, but he never listens. \n\nBut lately... I don't know, I just look out the window at them and it's like they belong to somebody else. Same thing with my workbench. I used to love sanding down old scrap wood, just making things smooth, you know? And football—I've been a Falcons fan since I was a boy. Had season tickets back in the nineties. But this past Sunday, I didn't even turn the game on. Even at church, I used to stay after and talk with folks for an hour, but now I just slip out the back as soon as the benediction is over. It's just... none of it feels like mine anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling more tired or having less energy lately, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: Tired? No, I can't say I've noticed that. I've got about as much energy as I usually do. I do drink a fair amount of coffee, though. Just regular black coffee from the breakroom. Gary actually just bought us one of those new machines, you know, the ones that take the little plastic pods? I told him it was a waste of money, but he insisted. The old glass pot we had kept leaking everywhere. I swear, I was getting the mop out three times a week just to clean up the linoleum. And let me tell you, the mops they send us from corporate now are just pure garbage. They just push the dirty water around instead of soaking it up. It's like trying to clean up a spill with a plastic bag. Back in the day, we had these thick cotton mop heads that could dry a whole puddle in one go... but, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being tired. Yeah, physically I feel fine, my energy hasn't really dropped or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Depressed? No, I wouldn't use that word. I'm not sitting around crying or feeling sorry for myself. Crying never did anyone any good anyway. My old man used to say that. He worked at the steel mill up in Birmingham for forty years. Forty years, breathing in all that dust and heat. He never complained once. Well, except about the price of gas. Man, I remember when gas went over a dollar a gallon, he practically blew a gasket. He drove this old Ford pickup—it used to be candy apple red, but it was all rusted out in the wheel wells by the time I was a teenager. He loved that truck, though. Kept the engine pristine. He used to make me hold the flashlight for him out in the dirt driveway while he tinkered with the carburetor, and if I didn't hold the beam exactly where he needed it, he'd just holler and holler... \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Oh, feeling down. No, I'm not really feeling sad or down like that. I just don't care about my tomatoes or the Falcons right now, like I said, but I'm not walking around moping all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving slower than usual or, on the contrary, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Well, I definitely haven't been restless. It's actually the opposite. People at work keep asking me if I'm coming down with something. They say I'm walking slower, and honestly, it feels like I'm moving through mud just trying to get from my desk to the warehouse floor. And taking forever to answer simple questions, too. Gary asked me about the inventory logs yesterday, and I just stared at him for a solid minute before I could even get a word out. \n\nSpeaking of Gary, he's been on this new health kick lately. Drinking these green smoothies that smell like fresh cut grass. He keeps trying to get me to try one, says it'll give me more energy. I told him I'd rather eat dirt. Oh, moving slower. Yeah, like I said, it feels like I'm walking through mud, and everyone at work is noticing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble sleeping lately, like waking up early or not being able to fall asleep, or on the flip side, have you been sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I've been sleeping my regular hours. Usually out like a light by 10:30. My wife, on the other hand, she's a very light sleeper. We actually had to get one of those sound machines, you know, the ones that play ocean waves or a rainstorm? I think it just sounds like static on an old tube TV. Remember those? The big heavy wooden boxes that took up half the living room. \n\nMy granddad had this huge Zenith console TV down in his den. Thing weighed an absolute ton. When the picture tube finally blew, he wouldn't let anyone throw it out. He just put a lace doily on top of it and used it as a plant stand. He had these massive Boston ferns that would drop little dried leaves everywhere. My grandma was always chasing after him with the Dustbuster. Man, those old Dustbusters... the batteries on those things were terrible. You'd leave it plugged into the wall all week, and you'd get maybe two minutes of suction before it started whining like a dying mosquito. They don't make appliances like they used to, that's for sure. Now everything is just cheap plastic from overseas. \n\nAnyway... wait, what did you ask me? Did you ask about my wife's sleep? Oh, right, my sleep. Yeah, no, my sleep is fine. I'm not waking up early or sleeping the day away or anything like that. Just regular sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder than usual to focus on tasks, make decisions, or think clearly over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Focus? Yeah, it's been real tough lately. It's like I can't put the pieces together. \n\nIt reminds me of when my youngest, Bobby, was trying to put together this Lego pirate ship I got him for his tenth birthday. Well, looking back on it, these low stretches... they've been coming around for about a year now. Just these times where I don't care much. We drove out there to help her move, me and the wife. Rented one of those big yellow Penske trucks. Driving that thing through the mountains was nerve-wracking, I'll tell you what. The brakes on those rental trucks always feel a little spongy to me. We stopped at this diner somewhere in Idaho, place looked like a giant potato from the outside. Best chicken fried steak I've ever had, though. The gravy was thick, real peppery, just how I like it. My doctor's always telling me to watch my cholesterol, lay off the gravy and the red meat. Oh, right, the low stretches. Yeah, they've been happening for about a year, but this time, since she moved, it's just been really, really tough to care about anything.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a time in your life before this past year when you felt this way—losing interest in things, feeling like you didn’t care, or having thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Before this past year? Hmm. You know, I don't really think so. Not like this. I mean, everybody gets a little down now and then, right? Like when the local team loses the playoffs. Boy, let me tell you, back in '08, when our boys missed the championship by one point... one point! The whole town was just in a funk for a month. 

I remember sitting at the sports bar down on 4th Street, O'Malley's, when that final buzzer went off. Place went dead silent. You could hear a pin drop. O'Malley, the owner, he just walked over to the TV and shut it off, didn't even say a word. He's a good guy, O'Malley. Always gave us a free basket of onion rings if we bought a pitcher. He had this recipe for the batter, used a dark stout beer in it. Best onion rings I've ever had. Not like those frozen ones you get at the grocery store. My wife brought home a bag of those once, tried to bake 'em in the oven instead of frying them. They came out all soggy and pale. I told her, "Martha, you can't bake an onion ring and expect it to taste right." You need a deep fryer. We used to have one, one of those little FryDaddys. Got it as a wedding gift. Used that thing for everything until the cord frayed. Almost started a fire in the kitchen. The smoke alarm went off, and the dog—we had a Golden Retriever named Buster back then—Buster started howling like a siren...

Wait, what did you ask me? Oh, feeling like this before last year. No, no, I don't think I ever felt this bad or stopped caring like this before this past year started. Not that I can remember, anyway.

But this one, right after my daughter left, this one has hit me the hardest. \n\nShe just moved out to Oregon, you know. Got a job with some tech company out in Portland. It's a long way from here. We drove out there to help her move, me and the wife. Rented one of those big yellow Penske trucks. Driving that thing through the mountains was nerve-wracking, I'll tell you what. The brakes on those rental trucks always feel a little spongy to me. We stopped at this diner somewhere in Idaho, place looked like a giant potato from the outside. Best chicken fried steak I've ever had, though. The gravy was thick, real peppery, just how I like it. My doctor's always telling me to watch my cholesterol, lay off the gravy and the red meat. Says I need to eat more leafy greens. I tried eating kale once. Tasted like dirt and cardboard mixed together. I don't understand how people eat that stuff on purpose. My neighbor, Gary, he grows it in his backyard. Gary's a nice guy, but he's always out there at 6 AM on a Saturday with his leaf blower. Who uses a leaf blower at 6 AM on a weekend? People are trying to sleep. I went over there once to ask him to keep it down, and he started talking my ear off about his new riding mower...\n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the low stretches. Yeah, they've been happening for about a year, but this time, since she moved, it's just been really, really tough to care about anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life before this past year when you felt this way—losing interest in things, feeling like you didn’t care, or having thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: Before this past year? Hmm. You know, I don't really think so. Not like this. I mean, everybody gets a little down now and then, right? Like when the local team loses the playoffs. Boy, let me tell you, back in '08, when our boys missed the championship by one point... one point! The whole town was just in a funk for a month. \n\nI remember sitting at the sports bar down on 4th Street, O'Malley's, when that final buzzer went off. Place went dead silent. You could hear a pin drop. O'Malley, the owner, he just walked over to the TV and shut it off, didn't even say a word. He's a good guy, O'Malley. Always gave us a free basket of onion rings if we bought a pitcher. He had this recipe for the batter, used a dark stout beer in it. Best onion rings I've ever had. Not like those frozen ones you get at the grocery store. My wife brought home a bag of those once, tried to bake 'em in the oven instead of frying them. They came out all soggy and pale. I told her, \"Martha, you can't bake an onion ring and expect it to taste right.\" You need a deep fryer. We used to have one, one of those little FryDaddys. Got it as a wedding gift. Used that thing for everything until the cord frayed. Almost started a fire in the kitchen. The smoke alarm went off, and the dog—we had a Golden Retriever named Buster back then—Buster started howling like a siren...\n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, feeling like this before last year. No, no, I don't think I ever felt this bad or stopped caring like this before this past year started. Not that I can remember, anyway.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a time in your life when you felt better for a few months and then started feeling this way again, or has this been the first time you've felt like this in a long while?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 57 / general_or_operations_manager / less_than_9th\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\nmanifestation: \"Before this low spell hit, I was either feeling way too fired up like everything was finally going my way or snapping at folks over small things I normally let slide.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, you know, it's funny you ask that. Before this whole low spell hit, maybe a few months before, I was actually feeling... I don't know, way too fired up. Like everything was finally going my way, you know? But at the same time, I was snapping at folks over small things I normally let slide. Like down at the warehouse. 

I've been managing that place for, gosh, going on fifteen years now. We move a lot of freight, mostly auto parts. It's a loud place, lots of forklifts beeping. We got these new electric forklifts a couple years ago. Supposed to be better for the environment, no exhaust fumes inside the building. But they're so quiet, you can't hear 'em coming around the corners. We had to install all these convex mirrors at every intersection just so guys wouldn't run into each other. I ordered them from this catalog, industrial supply place. The guy on the phone, his name was Kevin, he tried to upsell me on these fancy heated mirrors. I said, "Kevin, we're inside a climate-controlled warehouse, why do I need heated mirrors?" He didn't really have an answer for that. Just reading off a script, I guess. It's like when you call the cable company and they try to get you to add the premium movie channels. I don't even watch movies anymore. Mostly just the History Channel. They run those marathons of that show about the guys finding old junk in barns. I love that show. Makes me think about my grandfather's barn back in Ohio. He had this old tractor in there, a Farmall Cub. Red paint all rusted out. We used to pretend we were driving it...

What were we talking about? Oh, right. Feeling better for a few months. Yeah, before I crashed like this, I was just super revved up, feeling great but also getting really irritated with everyone over nothing.

And let me tell you, the mops they send us from corporate now are just pure garbage. They just push the dirty water around instead of soaking it up. It's like trying to clean up a spill with a plastic bag. Back in the day, we had these thick cotton mop heads that could dry a whole puddle in one go... but, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being tired. Yeah, physically I feel fine, my energy hasn't really dropped or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Depressed? No, I wouldn't use that word. I'm not sitting around crying or feeling sorry for myself. Crying never did anyone any good anyway. My old man used to say that. He worked at the steel mill up in Birmingham for forty years. Forty years, breathing in all that dust and heat. He never complained once. Well, except about the price of gas. Man, I remember when gas went over a dollar a gallon, he practically blew a gasket. He drove this old Ford pickup—it used to be candy apple red, but it was all rusted out in the wheel wells by the time I was a teenager. He loved that truck, though. Kept the engine pristine. He used to make me hold the flashlight for him out in the dirt driveway while he tinkered with the carburetor, and if I didn't hold the beam exactly where he needed it, he'd just holler and holler... \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Oh, feeling down. No, I'm not really feeling sad or down like that. I just don't care about my tomatoes or the Falcons right now, like I said, but I'm not walking around moping all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving slower than usual or, on the contrary, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Well, I definitely haven't been restless. It's actually the opposite. People at work keep asking me if I'm coming down with something. They say I'm walking slower, and honestly, it feels like I'm moving through mud just trying to get from my desk to the warehouse floor. And taking forever to answer simple questions, too. Gary asked me about the inventory logs yesterday, and I just stared at him for a solid minute before I could even get a word out. \n\nSpeaking of Gary, he's been on this new health kick lately. Drinking these green smoothies that smell like fresh cut grass. He keeps trying to get me to try one, says it'll give me more energy. I told him I'd rather eat dirt. My wife, she tried one of those juice cleanses back in '08, right before our daughter's wedding. She was so cranky for three days, I ended up sleeping on the couch just to get some peace. The couch isn't bad, actually. It's one of those big sectionals from Rooms To Go. We got it on sale on Labor Day. The delivery guys, though, they scuffed up the doorframe trying to get it inside. I had to get the wood putty out and fix it myself. I used to be pretty handy with that stuff, but like I said before, I haven't even looked at my workbench lately... \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, moving slower. Yeah, like I said, it feels like I'm walking through mud, and everyone at work is noticing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble sleeping lately, like waking up early or not being able to fall asleep, or on the flip side, have you been sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I've been sleeping my regular hours. Usually out like a light by 10:30. My wife, on the other hand, she's a very light sleeper. We actually had to get one of those sound machines, you know, the ones that play ocean waves or a rainstorm? I think it just sounds like static on an old tube TV. Remember those? The big heavy wooden boxes that took up half the living room. \n\nMy granddad had this huge Zenith console TV down in his den. Thing weighed an absolute ton. When the picture tube finally blew, he wouldn't let anyone throw it out. He just put a lace doily on top of it and used it as a plant stand. He had these massive Boston ferns that would drop little dried leaves everywhere. My grandma was always chasing after him with the Dustbuster. Man, those old Dustbusters... the batteries on those things were terrible. You'd leave it plugged into the wall all week, and you'd get maybe two minutes of suction before it started whining like a dying mosquito. They don't make appliances like they used to, that's for sure. Now everything is just cheap plastic from overseas. \n\nAnyway... wait, what did you ask me? Did you ask about my wife's sleep? Oh, right, my sleep. Yeah, no, my sleep is fine. I'm not waking up early or sleeping the day away or anything like that. Just regular sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder than usual to focus on tasks, make decisions, or think clearly over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Focus? Yeah, it's been real tough lately. Down at the distribution center, I'll be sitting there staring at the schedule or the routing sheet, and my mind just won't lock in. Even little staffing calls, like figuring out who's covering the loading dock, they feel way bigger than they ought to. It's like I can't put the pieces together. \n\nIt reminds me of when my youngest, Bobby, was trying to put together this Lego pirate ship I got him for his tenth birthday. Must have had a thousand pieces. Little tiny gray and black bricks scattered all over the living room rug. That rug was a nightmare to keep clean, by the way. Shag carpeting. Who thought that was a good idea? My wife picked it out, said it made the room look \"cozy.\" Cozy until you step on a Lego barefoot at two in the morning going for a glass of water. I swear, stepping on a Lego is worse than stepping on a rusty nail. I did that once, actually, stepped on a nail out by the old shed. Had to go get a tetanus shot. The nurse who gave it to me, she had the heaviest hand I've ever felt. My arm ached for a week. I couldn't even lift my bowling ball. We used to bowl every Thursday night down at Pin Strikes. I had a custom ball, swirl of blue and silver, weighed exactly sixteen pounds...\n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right, thinking clearly. Yeah, like I said, I just can't seem to focus on the schedules at work. My brain just won't lock onto what I'm looking at.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or weight recently?\n\nPatient: Appetite? Well, I haven't really had one, to be honest. Most days I have to force myself just to eat a few bites. Even the stuff I usually like, you know, the sandwiches my wife packs or the chili from the food truck outside the warehouse, it just sits there on my desk until I end up throwing it out. \n\nSpeaking of that food truck, the guy who runs it, Sal, he's been parking in the exact same spot by the loading dock for going on five years now. He’s got this bright yellow truck with a giant smiling taco painted on the side. The paint's starting to peel, though. You can see the old logo underneath, used to be an ice cream truck, I think. I remember when the ice cream man used to come through our neighborhood when I was a kid. You'd hear that little tinkling music from three blocks away and just take off running. My mom would give me a quarter, and that was enough for a Bomb Pop and a handful of those little wax bottles with the colored sugar water inside. I don't even know what that liquid was, probably pure chemicals. Nobody cared back then. We used to ride our bikes without helmets, drink from the garden hose... the hose water always tasted a little bit like rubber and dirt, but it was the best water you ever had when it was ninety degrees out. We had this big oak tree in the front yard we'd sit under to cool off. Lightning struck it back in '92, split the trunk right down the middle. Had to pay a guy five hundred bucks to come chop it up and haul it away. Five hundred bucks was a lot of money back then...\n\nWait, did you ask about my weight? Or food? Right, eating. Yeah, I just have to make myself eat lately, and most of it just goes in the trash.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period like this before, where you lost interest in things and had trouble focusing or felt like you didn’t care anymore?\n\nPatient: Have I had a period like this before? Well, looking back on it, these low stretches... they've been coming around for about a year now. Just these times where I don't care much. But this one, right after my daughter left, this one has hit me the hardest. \n\nShe just moved out to Oregon, you know. Got a job with some tech company out in Portland. It's a long way from here. We drove out there to help her move, me and the wife. Rented one of those big yellow Penske trucks. Driving that thing through the mountains was nerve-wracking, I'll tell you what. The brakes on those rental trucks always feel a little spongy to me. We stopped at this diner somewhere in Idaho, place looked like a giant potato from the outside. Best chicken fried steak I've ever had, though. The gravy was thick, real peppery, just how I like it. My doctor's always telling me to watch my cholesterol, lay off the gravy and the red meat. Says I need to eat more leafy greens. I tried eating kale once. Tasted like dirt and cardboard mixed together. I don't understand how people eat that stuff on purpose. My neighbor, Gary, he grows it in his backyard. Gary's a nice guy, but he's always out there at 6 AM on a Saturday with his leaf blower. Who uses a leaf blower at 6 AM on a weekend? People are trying to sleep. I went over there once to ask him to keep it down, and he started talking my ear off about his new riding mower...\n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the low stretches. Yeah, they've been happening for about a year, but this time, since she moved, it's just been really, really tough to care about anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life before this past year when you felt this way—losing interest in things, feeling like you didn’t care, or having thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: Before this past year? Hmm. You know, I don't really think so. Not like this. I mean, everybody gets a little down now and then, right? Like when the local team loses the playoffs. Boy, let me tell you, back in '08, when our boys missed the championship by one point... one point! The whole town was just in a funk for a month. \n\nI remember sitting at the sports bar down on 4th Street, O'Malley's, when that final buzzer went off. Place went dead silent. You could hear a pin drop. O'Malley, the owner, he just walked over to the TV and shut it off, didn't even say a word. He's a good guy, O'Malley. Always gave us a free basket of onion rings if we bought a pitcher. He had this recipe for the batter, used a dark stout beer in it. Best onion rings I've ever had. Not like those frozen ones you get at the grocery store. My wife brought home a bag of those once, tried to bake 'em in the oven instead of frying them. They came out all soggy and pale. I told her, \"Martha, you can't bake an onion ring and expect it to taste right.\" You need a deep fryer. We used to have one, one of those little FryDaddys. Got it as a wedding gift. Used that thing for everything until the cord frayed. Almost started a fire in the kitchen. The smoke alarm went off, and the dog—we had a Golden Retriever named Buster back then—Buster started howling like a siren...\n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, feeling like this before last year. No, no, I don't think I ever felt this bad or stopped caring like this before this past year started. Not that I can remember, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you felt better for a few months and then started feeling this way again, or has this been the first time you've felt like this in a long while?\n\nPatient: Well, you know, it's funny you ask that. Before this whole low spell hit, maybe a few months before, I was actually feeling... I don't know, way too fired up. Like everything was finally going my way, you know? But at the same time, I was snapping at folks over small things I normally let slide. Like down at the warehouse. \n\nI've been managing that place for, gosh, going on fifteen years now. We move a lot of freight, mostly auto parts. It's a loud place, lots of forklifts beeping. We got these new electric forklifts a couple years ago. Supposed to be better for the environment, no exhaust fumes inside the building. But they're so quiet, you can't hear 'em coming around the corners. We had to install all these convex mirrors at every intersection just so guys wouldn't run into each other. I ordered them from this catalog, industrial supply place. The guy on the phone, his name was Kevin, he tried to upsell me on these fancy heated mirrors. I said, \"Kevin, we're inside a climate-controlled warehouse, why do I need heated mirrors?\" He didn't really have an answer for that. Just reading off a script, I guess. It's like when you call the cable company and they try to get you to add the premium movie channels. I don't even watch movies anymore. Mostly just the History Channel. They run those marathons of that show about the guys finding old junk in barns. I love that show. Makes me think about my grandfather's barn back in Ohio. He had this old tractor in there, a Farmall Cub. Red paint all rusted out. We used to pretend we were driving it...\n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right. Feeling better for a few months. Yeah, before I crashed like this, I was just super revved up, feeling great but also getting really irritated with everyone over nothing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Not that I can remember, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you felt better for a few months and then started feeling this way again, or has this been the first time you've felt like this in a long while?\n\nPatient: Well, you know, it's funny you ask that. Before this whole low spell hit, maybe a few months before, I was actually feeling... I don't know, way too fired up. Like everything was finally going my way, you know? But at the same time, I was snapping at folks over small things I normally let slide. Like down at the warehouse. \n\nI've been managing that place for, gosh, going on fifteen years now. We move a lot of freight, mostly auto parts. It's a loud place, lots of forklifts beeping. We got these new electric forklifts a couple years ago. Supposed to be better for the environment, no exhaust fumes inside the building. But they're so quiet, you can't hear 'em coming around the corners. We had to install all these convex mirrors at every intersection just so guys wouldn't run into each other. I ordered them from this catalog, industrial supply place. The guy on the phone, his name was Kevin, he tried to upsell me on these fancy heated mirrors. I said, \"Kevin, we're inside a climate-controlled warehouse, why do I need heated mirrors?\" He didn't really have an answer for that. Just reading off a script, I guess. It's like when you call the cable company and they try to get you to add the premium movie channels. I don't even watch movies anymore. Mostly just the History Channel. They run those marathons of that show about the guys finding old junk in barns. I love that show. Makes me think about my grandfather's barn back in Ohio. He had this old tractor in there, a Farmall Cub. Red paint all rusted out. We used to pretend we were driving it...\n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right. Feeling better for a few months. Yeah, before I crashed like this, I was just super revved up, feeling great but also getting really irritated with everyone over nothing.\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you tell me more about that time when you were feeling really fired up and things seemed to be going your way—how long did that last, and were there specific things you were doing or feeling during that time?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 57 / general_or_operations_manager / less_than_9th\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Increased_Activity\nmanifestation: \"I had so much energy for a while that I was up before daylight planning warehouse changes, cleaning the garage, and jumping from one job to the next without wanting to stop.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, for a while there, I just had so much energy. It was wild. I’d be up way before daylight, like three or four in the morning, sitting at the kitchen table planning out all these big changes for the warehouse. Drawing up new floor plans, figuring out where to move the shipping bays so the trucks could back in easier. I'd be drinking my coffee while I did it. Just regular Folgers. I tried that fancy French roast stuff once, and it just tasted burnt to me. My brother-in-law drinks that dark roast. He lives up in Chicago, thinks he knows everything about coffee just because there’s a cafe on every corner up there. He came down for Thanksgiving a couple years back and actually brought his own beans in a little glass jar. Who travels with their own coffee beans? He’s got this little hand grinder too, made a terrible racket in the kitchen. Woke up the dog. 

But anyway, yeah, I was up way before all that. By the time the sun actually came up, I’d be out cleaning the garage top to bottom. Found my old half-inch socket wrench I thought I lost back in 2015. It was tucked behind a box of Martha's Christmas ornaments. We have so many of those things. She buys a new one every single year. Last year she got this little glass snowman, but the wire hook was loose and it fell right off the branch and shattered on the hardwood floor. I had to get the shop vac out so nobody would step on the glass. Shop vacs are great, but the filters get clogged so fast if you're sucking up sawdust...

Wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, what I was doing when I was fired up. Yeah, I was just jumping from one job to the next, all day long. The warehouse plans, cleaning the garage, fixing things. I just didn't want to stop at all. It lasted for a while like that, just going and going without needing a break.
