No, I can't say I've noticed that. I've got about as much energy as I usually do. I do drink a fair amount of coffee, though. Just regular black coffee from the breakroom. Gary actually just bought us one of those new machines, you know, the ones that take the little plastic pods? I told him it was a waste of money, but he insisted. The old glass pot we had kept leaking everywhere. I swear, I was getting the mop out three times a week just to clean up the linoleum. And let me tell you, the mops they send us from corporate now are just pure garbage. They just push the dirty water around instead of soaking it up. It's like trying to clean up a spill with a plastic bag. Back in the day, we had these thick cotton mop heads that could dry a whole puddle in one go... but, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being tired. Yeah, physically I feel fine, my energy hasn't really dropped or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Depressed? No, I wouldn't use that word. I'm not sitting around crying or feeling sorry for myself. Crying never did anyone any good anyway. My old man used to say that. He worked at the steel mill up in Birmingham for forty years. Forty years, breathing in all that dust and heat. He never complained once. Well, except about the price of gas. Man, I remember when gas went over a dollar a gallon, he practically blew a gasket. He drove this old Ford pickup—it used to be candy apple red, but it was all rusted out in the wheel wells by the time I was a teenager. He loved that truck, though. Kept the engine pristine. He used to make me hold the flashlight for him out in the dirt driveway while he tinkered with the carburetor, and if I didn't hold the beam exactly where he needed it, he'd just holler and holler... \n\nBut anyway, what were you asking? Oh, feeling down. No, I'm not really feeling sad or down like that. I just don't care about my tomatoes or the Falcons right now, like I said, but I'm not walking around moping all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving slower than usual or, on the contrary, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Well, I definitely haven't been restless. It's actually the opposite. People at work keep asking me if I'm coming down with something. They say I'm walking slower, and honestly, it feels like I'm moving through mud just trying to get from my desk to the warehouse floor. And taking forever to answer simple questions, too. Gary asked me about the inventory logs yesterday, and I just stared at him for a solid minute before I could even get a word out. \n\nSpeaking of Gary, he's been on this new health kick lately. Drinking these green smoothies that smell like fresh cut grass. He keeps trying to get me to try one, says it'll give me more energy. I told him I'd rather eat dirt. My wife, she tried one of those juice cleanses back in '08, right before our daughter's wedding. She was so cranky for three days, I ended up sleeping on the couch just to get some peace. The couch isn't bad, actually. It's one of those big sectionals from Rooms To Go. We got it on sale on Labor Day. The delivery guys, though, they scuffed up the doorframe trying to get it inside. I had to get the wood putty out and fix it myself. I used to be pretty handy with that stuff, but like I said before, I haven't even looked at my workbench lately... \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, moving slower. Yeah, like I said, it feels like I'm walking through mud, and everyone at work is noticing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any trouble sleeping lately, like waking up early or not being able to fall asleep, or on the flip side, have you been sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I've been sleeping my regular hours. Usually out like a light by 10:30. My wife, on the other hand, she's a very light sleeper. We actually had to get one of those sound machines, you know, the ones that play ocean waves or a rainstorm? I think it just sounds like static on an old tube TV. Remember those? The big heavy wooden boxes that took up half the living room. \n\nMy granddad had this huge Zenith console TV down in his den. Thing weighed an absolute ton. When the picture tube finally blew, he wouldn't let anyone throw it out. He just put a lace doily on top of it and used it as a plant stand. He had these massive Boston ferns that would drop little dried leaves everywhere. My grandma was always chasing after him with the Dustbuster. Man, those old Dustbusters... the batteries on those things were terrible. You'd leave it plugged into the wall all week, and you'd get maybe two minutes of suction before it started whining like a dying mosquito. They don't make appliances like they used to, that's for sure. Now everything is just cheap plastic from overseas. \n\nAnyway... wait, what did you ask me? Did you ask about my wife's sleep? Oh, right, my sleep. Yeah, no, my sleep is fine. I'm not waking up early or sleeping the day away or anything like that. Just regular sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder than usual to focus on tasks, make decisions, or think clearly over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Focus? Yeah, it's been real tough lately. Down at the distribution center, I'll be sitting there staring at the schedule or the routing sheet, and my mind just won't lock in. Even little staffing calls, like figuring out who's covering the loading dock, they feel way bigger than they ought to. It's like I can't put the pieces together. \n\nIt reminds me of when my youngest, Bobby, was trying to put together this Lego pirate ship I got him for his tenth birthday. Must have had a thousand pieces. Little tiny gray and black bricks scattered all over the living room rug. That rug was a nightmare to keep clean, by the way. Shag carpeting. Who thought that was a good idea? My wife picked it out, said it made the room look \"cozy.\" Cozy until you step on a Lego barefoot at two in the morning going for a glass of water. I swear, stepping on a Lego is worse than stepping on a rusty nail. I did that once, actually, stepped on a nail out by the old shed. Had to go get a tetanus shot. The nurse who gave it to me, she had the heaviest hand I've ever felt. My arm ached for a week. I couldn't even lift my bowling ball. We used to bowl every Thursday night down at Pin Strikes. I had a custom ball, swirl of blue and silver, weighed exactly sixteen pounds...\n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right, thinking clearly. Yeah, like I said, I just can't seem to focus on the schedules at work. My brain just won't lock onto what I'm looking at.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or weight recently?\n\nPatient: Appetite? Well, I haven't really had one, to be honest. Most days I have to force myself just to eat a few bites. Even the stuff I usually like, you know, the sandwiches my wife packs or the chili from the food truck outside the warehouse, it just sits there on my desk until I end up throwing it out. \n\nSpeaking of that food truck, the guy who runs it, Sal, he's been parking in the exact same spot by the loading dock for going on five years now. He’s got this bright yellow truck with a giant smiling taco painted on the side. The paint's starting to peel, though. You can see the old logo underneath, used to be an ice cream truck, I think. I remember when the ice cream man used to come through our neighborhood when I was a kid. You'd hear that little tinkling music from three blocks away and just take off running. My mom would give me a quarter, and that was enough for a Bomb Pop and a handful of those little wax bottles with the colored sugar water inside. I don't even know what that liquid was, probably pure chemicals. Nobody cared back then. We used to ride our bikes without helmets, drink from the garden hose... the hose water always tasted a little bit like rubber and dirt, but it was the best water you ever had when it was ninety degrees out. We had this big oak tree in the front yard we'd sit under to cool off. Lightning struck it back in '92, split the trunk right down the middle. Had to pay a guy five hundred bucks to come chop it up and haul it away. Five hundred bucks was a lot of money back then...\n\nWait, did you ask about my weight? Or food? Right, eating. Yeah, I just have to make myself eat lately, and most of it just goes in the trash.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period like this before, where you lost interest in things and had trouble focusing or felt like you didn’t care anymore?\n\nPatient: Have I had a period like this before? Well, looking back on it, these low stretches... they've been coming around for about a year now. Just these times where I don't care much. But this one, right after my daughter left, this one has hit me the hardest. \n\nShe just moved out to Oregon, you know. Got a job with some tech company out in Portland. It's a long way from here. We drove out there to help her move, me and the wife. Rented one of those big yellow Penske trucks. Driving that thing through the mountains was nerve-wracking, I'll tell you what. The brakes on those rental trucks always feel a little spongy to me. We stopped at this diner somewhere in Idaho, place looked like a giant potato from the outside. Best chicken fried steak I've ever had, though. The gravy was thick, real peppery, just how I like it. My doctor's always telling me to watch my cholesterol, lay off the gravy and the red meat. Says I need to eat more leafy greens. I tried eating kale once. Tasted like dirt and cardboard mixed together. I don't understand how people eat that stuff on purpose. My neighbor, Gary, he grows it in his backyard. Gary's a nice guy, but he's always out there at 6 AM on a Saturday with his leaf blower. Who uses a leaf blower at 6 AM on a weekend? People are trying to sleep. I went over there once to ask him to keep it down, and he started talking my ear off about his new riding mower...\n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the low stretches. Yeah, they've been happening for about a year, but this time, since she moved, it's just been really, really tough to care about anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life before this past year when you felt this way—losing interest in things, feeling like you didn’t care, or having thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: Before this past year? Hmm. You know, I don't really think so. Not like this. I mean, everybody gets a little down now and then, right? Like when the local team loses the playoffs. Boy, let me tell you, back in '08, when our boys missed the championship by one point... one point! The whole town was just in a funk for a month. \n\nI remember sitting at the sports bar down on 4th Street, O'Malley's, when that final buzzer went off. Place went dead silent. You could hear a pin drop. O'Malley, the owner, he just walked over to the TV and shut it off, didn't even say a word. He's a good guy, O'Malley. Always gave us a free basket of onion rings if we bought a pitcher. He had this recipe for the batter, used a dark stout beer in it. Best onion rings I've ever had. Not like those frozen ones you get at the grocery store. My wife brought home a bag of those once, tried to bake 'em in the oven instead of frying them. They came out all soggy and pale. I told her, \"Martha, you can't bake an onion ring and expect it to taste right.\" You need a deep fryer. We used to have one, one of those little FryDaddys. Got it as a wedding gift. Used that thing for everything until the cord frayed. Almost started a fire in the kitchen. The smoke alarm went off, and the dog—we had a Golden Retriever named Buster back then—Buster started howling like a siren...\n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, feeling like this before last year. No, no, I don't think I ever felt this bad or stopped caring like this before this past year started. Not that I can remember, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you felt better for a few months and then started feeling this way again, or has this been the first time you've felt like this in a long while?\n\nPatient: Well, you know, it's funny you ask that. Before this whole low spell hit, maybe a few months before, I was actually feeling... I don't know, way too fired up. Like everything was finally going my way, you know? But at the same time, I was snapping at folks over small things I normally let slide. Like down at the warehouse. \n\nI've been managing that place for, gosh, going on fifteen years now. We move a lot of freight, mostly auto parts. It's a loud place, lots of forklifts beeping. We got these new electric forklifts a couple years ago. Supposed to be better for the environment, no exhaust fumes inside the building. But they're so quiet, you can't hear 'em coming around the corners. We had to install all these convex mirrors at every intersection just so guys wouldn't run into each other. I ordered them from this catalog, industrial supply place. The guy on the phone, his name was Kevin, he tried to upsell me on these fancy heated mirrors. I said, \"Kevin, we're inside a climate-controlled warehouse, why do I need heated mirrors?\" He didn't really have an answer for that. Just reading off a script, I guess. It's like when you call the cable company and they try to get you to add the premium movie channels. I don't even watch movies anymore. Mostly just the History Channel. They run those marathons of that show about the guys finding old junk in barns. I love that show. Makes me think about my grandfather's barn back in Ohio. He had this old tractor in there, a Farmall Cub. Red paint all rusted out. We used to pretend we were driving it...\n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right. Feeling better for a few months. Yeah, before I crashed like this, I was just super revved up, feeling great but also getting really irritated with everyone over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about that time when you were feeling really fired up and things seemed to be going your way—how long did that last, and were there specific things you were doing or feeling during that time?\n\nPatient: Well, for a while there, I just had so much energy. It was wild. I’d be up way before daylight, like three or four in the morning, sitting at the kitchen table planning out all these big changes for the warehouse. Drawing up new floor plans, figuring out where to move the shipping bays so the trucks could back in easier. I'd be drinking my coffee while I did it. Just regular Folgers. I tried that fancy French roast stuff once, and it just tasted burnt to me. My brother-in-law drinks that dark roast. He lives up in Chicago, thinks he knows everything about coffee just because there’s a cafe on every corner up there. He came down for Thanksgiving a couple years back and actually brought his own beans in a little glass jar. Who travels with their own coffee beans? He’s got this little hand grinder too, made a terrible racket in the kitchen. Woke up the dog. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I was up way before all that. By the time the sun actually came up, I’d be out cleaning the garage top to bottom. Found my old half-inch socket wrench I thought I lost back in 2015. It was tucked behind a box of Martha's Christmas ornaments. We have so many of those things. She buys a new one every single year. Last year she got this little glass snowman, but the wire hook was loose and it fell right off the branch and shattered on the hardwood floor. I had to get the shop vac out so nobody would step on the glass. Shop vacs are great, but the filters get clogged so fast if you're sucking up sawdust...\n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, what I was doing when I was fired up. Yeah, I was just jumping from one job to the next, all day long. The warehouse plans, cleaning the garage, fixing things. I just didn't want to stop at all. It lasted for a while like that, just going and going without needing a break.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I told her, \"Martha, you can't bake an onion ring and expect it to taste right.\" You need a deep fryer. We used to have one, one of those little FryDaddys. Got it as a wedding gift. Used that thing for everything until the cord frayed. Almost started a fire in the kitchen. The smoke alarm went off, and the dog—we had a Golden Retriever named Buster back then—Buster started howling like a siren...\n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, feeling like this before last year. No, no, I don't think I ever felt this bad or stopped caring like this before this past year started. Not that I can remember, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you felt better for a few months and then started feeling this way again, or has this been the first time you've felt like this in a long while?\n\nPatient: Well, you know, it's funny you ask that. Before this whole low spell hit, maybe a few months before, I was actually feeling... I don't know, way too fired up. Like everything was finally going my way, you know? But at the same time, I was snapping at folks over small things I normally let slide. Like down at the warehouse. \n\nI've been managing that place for, gosh, going on fifteen years now. We move a lot of freight, mostly auto parts. It's a loud place, lots of forklifts beeping. We got these new electric forklifts a couple years ago. Supposed to be better for the environment, no exhaust fumes inside the building. But they're so quiet, you can't hear 'em coming around the corners. We had to install all these convex mirrors at every intersection just so guys wouldn't run into each other. I ordered them from this catalog, industrial supply place. The guy on the phone, his name was Kevin, he tried to upsell me on these fancy heated mirrors. I said, \"Kevin, we're inside a climate-controlled warehouse, why do I need heated mirrors?\" He didn't really have an answer for that. Just reading off a script, I guess. It's like when you call the cable company and they try to get you to add the premium movie channels. I don't even watch movies anymore. Mostly just the History Channel. They run those marathons of that show about the guys finding old junk in barns. I love that show. Makes me think about my grandfather's barn back in Ohio. He had this old tractor in there, a Farmall Cub. Red paint all rusted out. We used to pretend we were driving it...\n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right. Feeling better for a few months. Yeah, before I crashed like this, I was just super revved up, feeling great but also getting really irritated with everyone over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about that time when you were feeling really fired up and things seemed to be going your way—how long did that last, and were there specific things you were doing or feeling during that time?\n\nPatient: Well, for a while there, I just had so much energy. It was wild. I’d be up way before daylight, like three or four in the morning, sitting at the kitchen table planning out all these big changes for the warehouse. Drawing up new floor plans, figuring out where to move the shipping bays so the trucks could back in easier. I'd be drinking my coffee while I did it. Just regular Folgers. I tried that fancy French roast stuff once, and it just tasted burnt to me. My brother-in-law drinks that dark roast. He lives up in Chicago, thinks he knows everything about coffee just because there’s a cafe on every corner up there. He came down for Thanksgiving a couple years back and actually brought his own beans in a little glass jar. Who travels with their own coffee beans? He’s got this little hand grinder too, made a terrible racket in the kitchen. Woke up the dog. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I was up way before all that. By the time the sun actually came up, I’d be out cleaning the garage top to bottom. Found my old half-inch socket wrench I thought I lost back in 2015. It was tucked behind a box of Martha's Christmas ornaments. We have so many of those things. She buys a new one every single year. Last year she got this little glass snowman, but the wire hook was loose and it fell right off the branch and shattered on the hardwood floor. I had to get the shop vac out so nobody would step on the glass. Shop vacs are great, but the filters get clogged so fast if you're sucking up sawdust...\n\nWait, what did you ask me? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, absolutely. My brain was just... it was like a motor with the throttle stuck wide open. My thoughts were running so fast I would start with the warehouse layout, and then two seconds later I'm thinking about fixing up my old motorcycle, and then suddenly I'm onto my daughter's future, and I just couldn't slow any of it down. 

Speaking of my daughter, she just started dating this new fella, Greg. Now, Greg's a polite kid, always says "yes sir" and "no sir," but he drives this beat-up Pontiac Grand Am that sounds like it's misfiring on at least one cylinder. I went out to the driveway last month and told him, "Greg, you need to pull your spark plugs and check the gap." He just stared at me like I was speaking Chinese. I swear, kids these days don't know the first thing about basic maintenance. When I was his age, I was already helping my old man rebuild a carburetor right on the kitchen table. Smelled like gasoline in the house for a week. My mother was furious, let me tell you. She chased us out to the back porch with a broom. We had this big screened-in porch back then, overlooking a patch of woods. Used to sit out there in the summer and listen to the cicadas. They get so loud in August, almost deafening, but it's a good sound. Reminds me of being a kid...

Anyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, my thoughts racing. Yeah, they were just zooming from one thing to the next exactly like that. I couldn't put the brakes on if I tried. And sleep? I hardly felt like I needed any, to be honest. I was just moving too fast.

Drinking these green smoothies that smell like fresh cut grass. He keeps trying to get me to try one, says it'll give me more energy. I told him I'd rather eat dirt. My wife, she tried one of those juice cleanses back in '08, right before our daughter's wedding. She was so cranky for three days, I ended up sleeping on the couch just to get some peace. The couch isn't bad, actually. It's one of those big sectionals from Rooms To Go. We got it on sale on Labor Day. The delivery guys, though, they scuffed up the doorframe trying to get it inside. No, I've been sleeping my regular hours. Usually out like a light by 10:30. My wife, on the other hand, she's a very light sleeper. The big heavy wooden boxes that took up half the living room. \n\nMy granddad had this huge Zenith console TV down in his den. Thing weighed an absolute ton. When the picture tube finally blew, he wouldn't let anyone throw it out. He just put a lace doily on top of it and used it as a plant stand. He had these massive Boston ferns that would drop little dried leaves everywhere. My grandma was always chasing after him with the Dustbuster. Oh, right, my sleep. Yeah, no, my sleep is fine. I'm not waking up early or sleeping the day away or anything like that. Just regular sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder than usual to focus on tasks, make decisions, or think clearly over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Focus? Yeah, it's been real tough lately. Down at the distribution center, I'll be sitting there staring at the schedule or the routing sheet, and my mind just won't lock in. Even little staffing calls, like figuring out who's covering the loading dock, they feel way bigger than they ought to. It's like I can't put the pieces together. \n\nIt reminds me of when my youngest, Bobby, was trying to put together this Lego pirate ship I got him for his tenth birthday. Must have had a thousand pieces. Little tiny gray and black bricks scattered all over the living room rug. That rug was a nightmare to keep clean, by the way. Shag carpeting. Who thought that was a good idea? My wife picked it out, said it made the room look \"cozy.\" Cozy until you step on a Lego barefoot at two in the morning going for a glass of water. I swear, stepping on a Lego is worse than stepping on a rusty nail. I did that once, actually, stepped on a nail out by the old shed. Had to go get a tetanus shot. The nurse who gave it to me, she had the heaviest hand I've ever felt. My arm ached for a week. I couldn't even lift my bowling ball. We used to bowl every Thursday night down at Pin Strikes. I had a custom ball, swirl of blue and silver, weighed exactly sixteen pounds...\n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right, thinking clearly. Yeah, like I said, I just can't seem to focus on the schedules at work. My brain just won't lock onto what I'm looking at.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or weight recently?\n\nPatient: Appetite? Well, I haven't really had one, to be honest. Most days I have to force myself just to eat a few bites. Even the stuff I usually like, you know, the sandwiches my wife packs or the chili from the food truck outside the warehouse, it just sits there on my desk until I end up throwing it out. \n\nSpeaking of that food truck, the guy who runs it, Sal, he's been parking in the exact same spot by the loading dock for going on five years now. He’s got this bright yellow truck with a giant smiling taco painted on the side. The paint's starting to peel, though. You can see the old logo underneath, used to be an ice cream truck, I think. I remember when the ice cream man used to come through our neighborhood when I was a kid. You'd hear that little tinkling music from three blocks away and just take off running. My mom would give me a quarter, and that was enough for a Bomb Pop and a handful of those little wax bottles with the colored sugar water inside. I don't even know what that liquid was, probably pure chemicals. Nobody cared back then. We had this big oak tree in the front yard we'd sit under to cool off. Lightning struck it back in '92, split the trunk right down the middle. Boy, let me tell you, back in '08, when our boys missed the championship by one point... one point! The whole town was just in a funk for a month. \n\nI remember sitting at the sports bar down on 4th Street, O'Malley's, when that final buzzer went off. Place went dead silent. You could hear a pin drop. O'Malley, the owner, he just walked over to the TV and shut it off, didn't even say a word. He's a good guy, O'Malley. Always gave us a free basket of onion rings if we bought a pitcher. He had this recipe for the batter, used a dark stout beer in it. Best onion rings I've ever had. Not like those frozen ones you get at the grocery store. My wife brought home a bag of those once, tried to bake 'em in the oven instead of frying them. They came out all soggy and pale. I told her, \"Martha, you can't bake an onion ring and expect it to taste right.\" You need a deep fryer. We used to have one, one of those little FryDaddys. Got it as a wedding gift. Used that thing for everything until the cord frayed. Almost started a fire in the kitchen. The smoke alarm went off, and the dog—we had a Golden Retriever named Buster back then—Buster started howling like a siren...\n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, feeling like this before last year. No, no, I don't think I ever felt this bad or stopped caring like this before this past year started. Not that I can remember, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you felt better for a few months and then started feeling this way again, or has this been the first time you've felt like this in a long while?\n\nPatient: Well, you know, it's funny you ask that. Before this whole low spell hit, maybe a few months before, I was actually feeling... I don't know, way too fired up. Like everything was finally going my way, you know? But at the same time, I was snapping at folks over small things I normally let slide. Like down at the warehouse. \n\nI've been managing that place for, gosh, going on fifteen years now. We move a lot of freight, mostly auto parts. It's a loud place, lots of forklifts beeping. We got these new electric forklifts a couple years ago. Supposed to be better for the environment, no exhaust fumes inside the building. But they're so quiet, you can't hear 'em coming around the corners. We had to install all these convex mirrors at every intersection just so guys wouldn't run into each other. I ordered them from this catalog, industrial supply place. The guy on the phone, his name was Kevin, he tried to upsell me on these fancy heated mirrors. I said, \"Kevin, we're inside a climate-controlled warehouse, why do I need heated mirrors?\" He didn't really have an answer for that. Just reading off a script, I guess. It's like when you call the cable company and they try to get you to add the premium movie channels. I don't even watch movies anymore. Mostly just the History Channel. They run those marathons of that show about the guys finding old junk in barns. I love that show. Makes me think about my grandfather's barn back in Ohio. He had this old tractor in there, a Farmall Cub. Red paint all rusted out. We used to pretend we were driving it...\n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right. Feeling better for a few months. Yeah, before I crashed like this, I was just super revved up, feeling great but also getting really irritated with everyone over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about that time when you were feeling really fired up and things seemed to be going your way—how long did that last, and were there specific things you were doing or feeling during that time?\n\nPatient: Well, for a while there, I just had so much energy. It was wild. I’d be up way before daylight, like three or four in the morning, sitting at the kitchen table planning out all these big changes for the warehouse. Drawing up new floor plans, figuring out where to move the shipping bays so the trucks could back in easier. I'd be drinking my coffee while I did it. Just regular Folgers. I tried that fancy French roast stuff once, and it just tasted burnt to me. My brother-in-law drinks that dark roast. He lives up in Chicago, thinks he knows everything about coffee just because there’s a cafe on every corner up there. He came down for Thanksgiving a couple years back and actually brought his own beans in a little glass jar. Who travels with their own coffee beans? He’s got this little hand grinder too, made a terrible racket in the kitchen. Woke up the dog. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I was up way before all that. By the time the sun actually came up, I’d be out cleaning the garage top to bottom. Found my old half-inch socket wrench I thought I lost back in 2015. It was tucked behind a box of Martha's Christmas ornaments. We have so many of those things. She buys a new one every single year. Last year she got this little glass snowman, but the wire hook was loose and it fell right off the branch and shattered on the hardwood floor. I had to get the shop vac out so nobody would step on the glass. Shop vacs are great, but the filters get clogged so fast if you're sucking up sawdust...\n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, what I was doing when I was fired up. Yeah, I was just jumping from one job to the next, all day long. The warehouse plans, cleaning the garage, fixing things. I just didn't want to stop at all. It lasted for a while like that, just going and going without needing a break.\n\nDoctor: During that time when you were feeling really energized and productive, did you ever feel like your thoughts were racing, or that you didn’t need much sleep, or maybe that others around you couldn’t keep up with you?\n\nPatient: Oh, absolutely. My brain was just... it was like a motor with the throttle stuck wide open. My thoughts were running so fast I would start with the warehouse layout, and then two seconds later I'm thinking about fixing up my old motorcycle, and then suddenly I'm onto my daughter's future, and I just couldn't slow any of it down. \n\nSpeaking of my daughter, she just started dating this new fella, Greg. Now, Greg's a polite kid, always says \"yes sir\" and \"no sir,\" but he drives this beat-up Pontiac Grand Am that sounds like it's misfiring on at least one cylinder. I went out to the driveway last month and told him, \"Greg, you need to pull your spark plugs and check the gap.\" He just stared at me like I was speaking Chinese. I swear, kids these days don't know the first thing about basic maintenance. When I was his age, I was already helping my old man rebuild a carburetor right on the kitchen table. Smelled like gasoline in the house for a week. My mother was furious, let me tell you. She chased us out to the back porch with a broom. We had this big screened-in porch back then, overlooking a patch of woods. Used to sit out there in the summer and listen to the cicadas. They get so loud in August, almost deafening, but it's a good sound. Reminds me of being a kid...\n\nAnyway, what were you asking? Oh, right, my thoughts racing. Yeah, they were just zooming from one thing to the next exactly like that. I couldn't put the brakes on if I tried. And sleep? I hardly felt like I needed any, to be honest. I was just moving too fast."
